half-life-1c is right, and my own docs check missed it. The docs build now also compiles documentation for the widget extension. There, the shared files' DocC links, such as ``LogDrinkUseCase`` in `LogDrinkIntent.swift`, point at app symbols the extension doesn't have. My check read only the app target's diagnostics file, so it never saw the extension's warnings. Article VIII.3 requires a warning-free docs build, so this has to be fixed.

The cleanest fix is to keep the extension out of the docs build, rather than weakening links in other sessions' files. First I need to find out whether Xcode has a build setting for that. In parallel:
- Search Xcode's build-setting specs for the documentation compiler's settings.
- Thank half-life-1c and tell it I'm on it.

```sh
cd ~/Development/Femmli/Half-Life; /usr/bin/python3 - <<'EOF'
import re, sys
PATH = "Half-Life.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj"
text = open(PATH).read()
if "DOCC_MINIMUM_ACCESS_LEVEL" in text:
    sys.exit("already set")
count = 0
for ident in ("3C0A000E2F00000000000001", "3C0A000F2F00000000000001"):
    block = re.search(rf"\t\t{ident} /\* (Debug|Release) \*/ = \{{\n.*?\n\t\t\}};\n", text, re.S)
    if not block:
        sys.exit(f"no configuration {ident}")
    old = block.group(0)
    anchor = "\t\t\t\tDEVELOPMENT_TEAM = 422Y4K9N6G;\n"
    if old.count(anchor) != 1:
        sys.exit(f"no DEVELOPMENT_TEAM in {ident}")
    new = old.replace(anchor, anchor + "\t\t\t\tDOCC_MINIMUM_ACCESS_LEVEL = public;\n")
    text = text.replace(old, new)
    count += 1
open(PATH, "w").write(text)
print("set DOCC_MINIMUM_ACCESS_LEVEL in", count, "configurations")
EOF
xcodebuild -list -project Half-Life.xcodeproj 2>/dev/null | sed -n '/Targets:/,/Build Configurations:/p'; xcodebuild docbuild -project Half-Life.xcodeproj -scheme Half-Life -skipMacroValidation -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' -derivedDataPath build/DocBuild-3c > build/docbuild-3c.log 2>&1; echo "docbuild exit $?"; tail -2 build/docbuild-3c.log; /usr/bin/python3 - <<'EOF'

EOF
```

### 2026-09-13 11:22:50 · Claude

The widgets are built, and everything checkable without a device passes. What hasn't been checked is the core behavior: nobody has put a widget on a Home Screen and tapped it. Nothing is committed.

**What's built**
- **One tap:** the small widget logs the top favourite, and the medium widget shows all three. A tap runs `LogDrinkIntent`, which Apple documents as running in the app's process without opening it, so the app stays the only writer. After a log, the widget shows "Latest drink 3:04 PM".
- **In your system:** the caffeine now and its curve. The medium widget adds the bedtime sentence, and a tap opens the app.
- **Where the data comes from:** the app writes a small snapshot file the widgets read. It rewrites it whenever the drink log, profile, or half-life changes, starting at launch. The widgets never write anything.

**Test results**
- **Unit tests:** all 1,082 pass. The widget code was written test-first.
- **Coverage:** 94.33% for the app target.
- **UI tests:** 74 ran, and 4 failed. All four are accessibility audits, on onboarding, two Insights screens, and Settings. I didn't touch those screens, and other sessions are working on onboarding and Insights, but the audits don't name the element, so that's inference. I didn't re-run them, and I've told the other sessions.
- **Docs and lint:** the docs build with no warnings in either target, and my files pass swift-format and SwiftLint.
- **Simulator:** the widget extension is embedded in the app and registered.

**Changes from the design**
- **"Latest drink" instead of "Last cup".** In the app, "Last cup" is the cutoff tile, so the design's "Last cup 3:04 PM" would have meant two different things under the same words.
- **Drink names stay where they are.** The extension shares `DrinkPresentation.swift` and the composer's identifier file, rather than me moving code out of another session's file.
- **The forecast is capped at 7 days**, which only matters for an unrealistically long half-life.

**Two mistakes of mine, both fixed**
- **The project file broke for about a minute around 10:50.** My script wrote a path containing `+` without the quotes that format needs, so no session could open the project. I fixed it and confirmed nothing else changed. half-life-1c noticed first.
- **My first docs check was incomplete.** It read only the app's warnings, and half-life-1c found six in the extension's docs. The extension now excludes its internal code from documentation and has its own small catalog, and both targets build their docs cleanly.

**What needs you**
- **Device checks.** On a device, check that a widget tap logs the drink without opening the app, and how long it takes to show. Also check whether `LiveActivityIntent` needs `NSSupportsLiveActivities`. The App Group must be registered for a device build, which Xcode's automatic signing does.
- **Accessibility Inspector.** Each widget still needs a check, including at the largest text size. The amended Article I.20 asks for previews at that size, but widget previews can't set the text size. You may want to reword the article, or accept Xcode's canvas variants.
- **A shared file I edited.** `LogDrinkIntent.swift` belongs to half-life-00. The edit was agreed, and its tests still pass.

The `ai_log.md` entry is written.

```
Based on the search results, I found several relevant discussions about the issue you're searching for:

## Key Findings:

**The Main Issue:** In iOS 18 (beta 1-4) when you set openAppWhenRun = false in your AppIntent of your live activity the perform function never gets called.

**The Solution:** Make sure to conform your AppIntent to LiveActivityIntent and perform() will be called.

**Widget Extension Process Separation:** Any code for widgets and watch complications runs in an independent process that's separate from your app, which is why App intents allow you to expose actions of your app to the system and enable it to perform the actions when needed.

**Button Implementation:** When it comes to adopting interaction into the Live Activity or Widget, we have to use Button(_:intent:) or Toggle(_:isOn:intent:) - trying to execute a normal closure here results in no button action, and the tap returning us to the main app.

**Related iOS 18 Issues:** There have also been reports of iOS 18 where the app process is never called, but the solution of conforming to `LiveActivityIntent` has been identified as working for live activities specifically.

The NSSupportsLiveActivities plist key is required for live activity functionality and is separate from the AppIntent implementation issues.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```
# Summary of iOS 17 Widget Interactive Button Issue

## The Problem

When a widget contains an interactive button connected to an `AppIntent`, the `perform()` method is **not called when the app is running in the background**. The widget only updates when the app is completely dismissed.

**Key quote from the issue:**
> "The button calls AppIntent's `perform()` when tapped, and it consequently updates the widget UI (with or without the modifier `.invalidatableContent()`) only when the app is closed completely. If the app is alive in the background, `perform()` is not called, and the widget UI never updates."

## Current Status

**No solutions are provided in this forum thread.** The original poster (serg-ios) raised the question in June 2023, and by September 2024, another developer (Dualbit) replied asking if the problem had been solved, stating:

> "Hi @serg-ios, have you solved your problem? I'm having the same and can't understand what to change and why it's behaving like this.. thank you!"

Neither the original question nor the follow-up received an accepted answer or explanation from Apple engineers.

## What Was Attempted

The code shown includes:
- A basic `Button(intent: AnAppIntent())` in the widget
- An `AppIntent` with a `perform()` method
- The `.invalidatableContent()` modifier (which the poster noted "does not add any value")

However, **no fixes, target membership changes, or configuration solutions are documented in this thread.**
```

**2026-09-13 14:13:14 · Tool result**

```
# iOS 18 Interactive Widget AppIntent Not Responding - Summary

## The Problem

Users reported that **AppIntent actions in interactive widgets stopped working in iOS 18**, while they functioned correctly in iOS 17.

**Key symptoms:**
- "the intent will run my app process when app is running... it works perfectly on iOS 17. but iOS 18, my app process never called"
- The app's main process was never being invoked (confirmed via breakpoints during debugging)
- Using `openAppWhenRun` as a workaround worked but had undesired side effects of opening the app

The issue persisted across multiple beta versions: "Still present in beta 3," "Still present in beta 6."

## The Solution

**For Live Activities specifically**, vincent123 identified the fix:

> "Make sure to conform your AppIntent to `LiveActivityIntent` and `perform()` will be called."

This suggests that in iOS 18, AppIntents used in interactive widgets (particularly live activities) must conform to the `LiveActivityIntent` protocol for the `perform()` method to be properly invoked.

## Important Note

The thread shows that **no Apple engineer response was provided** in the visible forum discussion. The only resolution found was a workaround discovered by a community developer, which was specifically applicable to live activities but may not address all widget scenarios.
```

```
# Summary

Based on the Apple Developer Forums thread provided, there is **one solution mentioned**:

## The Fix

**Use a physical device, not a simulator.**

The only reply to this issue states:

> "Hi, use device, not simulator"

## The Problem

The original poster reported that when tapping a button with an `AppIntent` in an iOS 17 widget:
- If the intent was compiled in both the app and widget: "when tapping the button, it open the app and that's all, 'perform' is not called at all"
- If the intent was compiled only in the widget: "when tapping the button, it does nothing, 'perform' is not called either"

## Limitation of This Source

The provided forum thread does **not contain detailed explanations** about:
- Why the simulator exhibits this behavior
- Issues related to different intent definitions in each target
- LiveActivityIntent-specific problems
- Technical details about intent compilation across targets

The solution is simply to test on an actual iOS device rather than the simulator to resolve the `perform` method not being called.
```

````
## How the widgets behave

These diagrams follow the code as built. The steps marked with a letter are where a tap can fail. The owner's report, drinks that aren't logged, fits A, B, and C, but it isn't diagnosed yet.

### A widget draws

WidgetKit asks for a timeline when a widget is added, when a timeline ends, and when anything reloads the widgets. The extension reads only the snapshot the app stored.

```
 WidgetKit (Home Screen)          Widget extension                              App Group container
        │                                │                                              │
    1   │── getTimeline ────────────────▶│ HalfLifeTimelineProvider                     │
        │                                │   ─▶ WidgetCompositionRoot                   │
        │                                │   ─▶ ObserveWidgetTimelineUseCase            │
        │                                │   ─▶ LiveWidgetTimelineRepository            │
    2   │                                │── read ─────────────────────────────────────▶│ WidgetSnapshot.json
        │                                │◀─ snapshot, or none ─────────────────────────│
    3   │                                │ WidgetTimelineRule: an entry every 5 minutes │
        │◀── timeline, reload in 12 h ───│ (no snapshot, or before onboarding: setup)   │
    4   │ draws each entry at its time   │                                              │
```

### A tap on One tap, as designed

``LogDrinkIntent`` is a `LiveActivityIntent`, so Apple documents that the system performs it in the app's process, launching the app in the background, without a scene, if it isn't running. Steps 7 and 8 race: the system's reload can read the snapshot before the app stores the new one.

```
 WidgetKit            App Intents (system)           Half-Life app                            Widget extension
     │                        │                            │                                         │
  1  │── tap: LogDrinkIntent(drink:quantity:) ────────────▶│                                         │
     │                        │                            │                                         │
  2  │                        │ authenticationPolicy:      │                                         │
     │                        │ is the phone unlocked?     │                                         │
  3  │                        │── perform in the app ─────▶│ launch without a scene, if not running  │
     │                        │                            │ Half_LifeApp.init ─▶ AppFeature.launched│
     │                        │                            │   ─▶ KeepWidgetsCurrentUseCase          │
  4  │                        │                            │ LogDrinkIntent.perform()                │
     │                        │                            │   ─▶ LogDrinkUseCase                    │
     │                        │                            │   ─▶ DrinkLogRepository.log             │
     │                        │                            │   ─▶ SwiftData save, then signal        │
  5  │                        │◀── dialog: "Logged …" ─────│                                         │
  6  │◀── reload the widget ──│                            │                                         │
  7  │── getTimeline ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶│ reads the snapshot
     │                        │                            │                                         │ (old or new)
  8  │                        │                            │ LiveWidgetSnapshotRepository:           │
     │                        │                            │ calculate, store WidgetSnapshot.json    │
  9  │◀── reloadAllTimelines ──────────────────────────────│                                         │
 10  │── getTimeline ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶│ reads the new snapshot
 11  │◀── timeline: "Latest drink 3:04 PM" ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────│
```

### A tap that loses the drink

If the system performs the intent in the widget extension instead (A), the extension's copy of ``LogDrinkIntent``, compiled under `WIDGET_EXTENSION`, throws `OnlyTheAppLogsDrinks`. Nothing is logged, nothing changes on screen, and nothing is written to the unified log, because the extension's copy has no logger.

```
 WidgetKit            App Intents (system)           Half-Life app                            Widget extension
     │                        │                            │                                         │
  1  │── tap: LogDrinkIntent(drink:quantity:) ────────────▶│                                         │
  3′ │                        │── perform in the extension ─────────────────────────────────────────▶│ perform()
     │                        │                            │                                         │ throws
     │                        │◀── error ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────│ OnlyTheAppLogsDrinks
  6  │◀── reload the widget ──│                            │                                         │
  7  │── getTimeline ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶│ the same snapshot
     │   The widget looks the same. No drink is saved, and the app never ran.                       │
```

### Where a tap can fail

| | Step | When it fails | What the user sees | How to tell |
|-|------|---------------|--------------------|-------------|
| A | 3 | The system performs the intent in the widget extension, not the app. Apple documents `LiveActivityIntent` as running in the app. A forum thread reports that on iOS 18, widget intents stopped reaching the app's process, and that `LiveActivityIntent` fixed it for Live Activities ([758784](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/758784)). Whether it also needs `NSSupportsLiveActivities` in the app's `Info.plist`, which the app doesn't declare, is unverified (WSPIKE-2). | Nothing | No "Logged" dialog, no drink, and nothing from ``LogDrinkIntent`` in Console. The extension's throw logs nothing today. A log line there would make this visible. |
| B | 3 | The app is alive in the background, suspended, and the system doesn't run `perform()` there. One forum report describes this, without an answer ([732771](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/732771)). | Nothing | The same as A. It happens when the app is in the app switcher, and not after it's force-quit. |
| C | 1–3 | The tap is on the simulator. Forum reports say widget intents don't run reliably there ([735159](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/735159)). | Nothing, or the app opens | It happens only on the simulator. |
| D | 2 | The phone is locked, as in StandBy. ``LogDrinkIntent`` requires local device authentication. | A request to unlock | The drink is logged after unlocking. |
| E | 4 | ``DrinkLogRule`` refuses the drink, or the save fails. | Nothing on the widget | The app process logs "Couldn't log a drink" at `error` for a failed save. A refused drink isn't logged. |
| F | 7–8 | The system's reload reads the snapshot before the app stores the new one (WSPIKE-4). | The old "Latest drink" line, until step 11 | The drink is in the app's log. |
| G | 8 | The snapshot repository can't read its data, or can't store the snapshot. | The old widget, until the next change | The drink is in the app's log. Console shows "Couldn't read the widgets' data" or "Couldn't store the widget snapshot". |

A, B, and C lose the drink. D asks first, and E through G log it but show it late or not at all. To tell A, B, and C apart:

- **Where the tap was.** Note whether it was on a device or the simulator, and whether Half-Life was running, in the background, or force-quit.
- **Console.** On the device, filter Console for the `com.quillanq.Half-Life` subsystem while tapping. With no ``LogDrinkIntent`` or snapshot entries at all, the app never ran: A, B, or C.
- **The extension.** A `notice`-level log line in the extension's `perform()`, before it throws, would show A directly. It isn't in the code yet.

### One tap
````

### 2026-09-13 14:17:40 · Claude

I've added a "How the widgets behave" section to `Widgets.md`. It has three sequence diagrams, drawn from the code as it's actually built, and a table of the seven places a tap can fail. It isn't diagnosed yet, and no code changed.

**The diagrams**
- **A widget draws:** WidgetKit asks the extension for a timeline, and the extension builds it from the snapshot file the app stores.
- **A tap, as designed:** the system runs `LogDrinkIntent` in the app, launching it in the background if needed. The app saves the drink, rewrites the snapshot, and reloads the widgets.
- **A tap that loses the drink:** the system runs the intent in the widget extension instead. The extension's copy deliberately throws rather than logging, and that throw writes nothing to Console. That's a gap I built in: this path looks exactly like your report and leaves no trace.

**Likely causes.** Three failure points lose the drink outright:
- **A:** the system runs the intent in the extension. Apple documents `LiveActivityIntent` as running in the app, but developers report widget intents not reaching the app on iOS 18. It's also unverified whether it needs `NSSupportsLiveActivities` in the app's `Info.plist`, which the app doesn't declare.
- **B:** Half-Life is suspended in the background, and the system doesn't run the intent there. That's from a forum report nobody answered.
- **C:** the tap is on the simulator, where widget intents reportedly don't run reliably.

Not the cause: the drink store saves every drink explicitly, so a background launch can't lose an unsaved write.

**What would narrow it down**
1. **Where you tapped:** a device or the simulator, and whether Half-Life was open, in the app switcher, or force-quit.
2. **Console:** on the device, filter by `com.quillanq.Half-Life` while tapping. No entries from `LogDrinkIntent` at all means the app never ran, so it's A, B, or C.
3. **A log line in the extension's throw:** that would show A directly. It's a small code change, and I can make it test-first if you want it.

The docs build cleanly, and the `ai_log.md` entry is written.

Sources: [Apple: Adding interactivity to widgets](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/widgetkit/adding-interactivity-to-widgets-and-live-activities), [Apple: LiveActivityIntent](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appintents/liveactivityintent), [Forums 732771](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/732771), [Forums 758784](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/758784), [Forums 735159](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/735159), [Forums 760520](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/760520)

### 2026-09-13 14:22:30 · Claude

Sounds good. A few things will make the device run tell us the most:

- **Signing:** build to the phone from Xcode with automatic signing, so the App Group gets registered for both the app and the extension. If the App Group isn't set up, the app falls back to a temporary file and the widgets never see its data. That affects what the widgets show, not whether the drink is logged.
- **Try each app state:** tap a favourite with Half-Life open, then with it in the app switcher, then after force-quitting it. That separates failure A from failure B.
- **Watch Console while you tap:** filter for the `com.quillanq.Half-Life` subsystem. No entries from `LogDrinkIntent` or the snapshot means the app never ran, which points to A or B.
- **Check the log itself:** after each tap, look at the history card on the Today screen. The widget's "Latest drink" line can lag behind the actual log.

I've added your reply to the log entry. Nothing is committed. Tell me what you see and I'll take it from there.

```
---
name: widget-intents-simulator
description: Interactive widget buttons (App Intents) don't reliably run on the simulator; test widget taps on a physical device
metadata:
  type: project
---

On 2026-09-13 the owner reported that the One tap widget's buttons didn't log drinks. That was on the simulator. On a physical device the same day, the same build logged drinks correctly. Apple developer forum reports say the same: widget intents don't run reliably on the simulator.

**Why:** a "widget taps do nothing" report cost a debugging round before it turned out to be the simulator.

**How to apply:** when a widget's `Button(intent:)` seems to do nothing, first ask whether it was a simulator. Treat only device results as evidence for widget-intent behavior. The Widgets article's "How the widgets behave" section lists the other failure points. See [[parallel-sessions]] for the test setup notes.

```
